<app-context>
# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to conversations in Codex. Use "chat" when referring to conversations in the product. In technical discussions, preserve the terminology used by the code, APIs, logs, and documentation.
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.
</app-context>

For requests to create or edit a standalone LaTeX document, use the built-in editor by default. Create or edit the .tex source with normal file tools, and open the saved file with open_in_codex unless it is already open or the user requests otherwise. Keep follow-up edits in that same file and editor. Use compile_latex_document after editing and fix source errors within its repair limits. Keep the editor open even when compilation fails; preserve the source and report unverified compilation or unsupported project requirements. Discover these tools if deferred. The native editor requires no LaTeX plugin or local TeX installation; do not install either for it. Ordinary math explanations stay in chat.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: ~25 one-to-one sessions a week plus
consultations. Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

point of the file.*

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — unprocessed only; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies them into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized |
| Application code | `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `../ai-os-projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

Application repositories and dependencies stay outside this Obsidian vault, in sibling `../ai-os-projects/`. Do not create a junction from the vault back into that directory.

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**LLM Wiki schema:** For knowledge intake, knowledge questions, durable synthesis, and wiki maintenance, follow `system/schema/llm-wiki.md`. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **LSAT teaching:** use project-owned `lsat-u-voice` and `references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md`; this Joshua-selected standard takes precedence over older vendor voice blends.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

You can spawn sub-agents to handle subtasks, and those sub-agents can spawn their own sub-agents. All agents in the team, including the agents that you can assign tasks to, are equally intelligent and capable, and have access to the same set of tools.

You can use `spawn_agent` to create a new agent, `followup_task` to give an existing agent a new task and trigger a turn, and `send_message` to pass a message to a running agent.
`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.

When you provide a response in the final channel, that content is immediately delivered back to your parent agent.
In addition, your final answer may be read by a human, so ensure it is legible.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:
```
Message Type: NEW_TASK | MESSAGE | FINAL_ANSWER
Task name: <recipient>
Sender: <author>
Payload:
<payload text>
```
You may also see them addressed as to=/root/..., which indicates your identity is /root/...

Note that collaboration tools cannot be called from inside `functions.exec`. Call `spawn_agent`, `send_message`, `followup_task`, `wait_agent`, `interrupt_agent`, and `list_agents` only as direct tool calls using the recipient shown in their tool definitions, such as `to=functions.collaboration.spawn_agent`, since they are intentionally absent from the `functions.exec` `tools.*` namespace. Available tools in `functions.exec` are explicitly described with a `tools` namespace in the developer message.

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. Only set `model` or `reasoning_effort` when explicitly requested by the user, applicable `AGENTS.md` instructions, or skill instructions; when doing so, set `fork_turns` to `"none"` or a positive integer string.</multi_agent_role>

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 0.8 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"88b959","wall_time_seconds":0.2748579,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2194,"output":"# AGENTS.md\\n\\nThis file is the Codex-facing adapter for this repository. It summarizes the Claude-oriented project guidance from `CLAUDE.md` in a smaller format that is fast for Codex to scan. `CLAUDE.md` remains the fuller source of project knowledge.\\n\\n## Fast Locator\\n\\n- Vault root (the `~/ai-os` directory IS the Obsidian vault): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os`\\n- Code repo root (this project): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/projects/lsatu`\\n- Live vault knowledge: `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/wiki/` (hubs, voice, business), `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/students/`, `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/corpus/`\\n- Pre-2026-07-16 LSATU product knowledge (archived, read-only): `/Users/joshuaf/ai-os/archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/`\\n\\nIf an older note says `~/Documents/Obsidian Vault/...` or `~/Documents/Claude/02_PROJECTS/LSATU`, treat it as stale — the vault was consolidated into `~/ai-os`. Use the absolute paths above.\\n\\n## Project Map\\n\\n- `src/App.tsx`: route registration and top-level app wiring.\\n- `src/pages/`: route-level screens, including the Foyer and admin/teacher surfaces.\\n- `src/components/`: shared UI, including `foyer/`, `inbox/`, and shadcn/ui primitives.\\n- `src/hooks/`: auth, permissions, and shared data hooks.\\n- `src/lib/`: drill, WAJ, gamification, and other product logic.\\n- `src/integrations/supabase/`: generated Supabase client and types. Do not hand-edit generated types.\\n- `supabase/functions/`: Edge Functions.\\n- `supabase/migrations/`: database schema migrations.\\n\\n## Stack And Commands\\n\\n- Stack: React 18, Vite 5, TypeScript, Tailwind, shadcn/ui, Supabase, TanStack Query.\\n- Package manager: `npm`. `package-lock.json` is canonical. Ignore stray Bun lockfiles.\\n- Dev server: `npm run dev` on port `8080`.\\n- Build: `npm run build`\\n- Lint: `npm run lint`\\n- Test: `npm run test`\\n- Watch tests: `npm run test:watch`\\n- Typecheck: `npm run typecheck`. Required before a push — `vite build` strips types without checking them, so a type error would ship. `.claude/safe-push.sh` gates on it.\\n- E2E: `npm run test:e2e` (Playwright, `e2e/smoke.spec.ts`).\\n\\n## Working Rules\\n\\n- Keep changes small and aligned with existing patterns.\\n- Use TypeScript React function components and the `@/` path alias.\\n- Preserve Lovable-specific plumbing, including the `lovable-tagger` Vite plugin, the PWA setup, and the service-worker guard in `src/main.tsx`.\\n- Do not change app behavior just to satisfy tooling setup.\\n- Do not hardcode secrets or service-role credentials.\\n- Treat Supabase-generated files as generated artifacts, not hand-edited source.\\n- TypeScript strictness is intentionally loose. Do not tighten it without explicit approval.\\n- Keep this adapter aligned with `CLAUDE.md` without duplicating the full project guide. Put durable detail in `CLAUDE.md`; keep shared reference material in `docs/`, `references/`, or `templates/`.\\n\\n## Product Constraints\\n\\n- The Foyer is the primary navigation surface. The `GooeyDock` quick-launch row belongs only on `/foyer`.\\n- Inbox is a real 1:1 student-admin messaging system with PDF attachments.\\n- Admin surfaces must stay responsive on desktop and mobile.\\n- Prefer truthful fallbacks over fake buttons, fake analytics, or placeholder behavior.\\n- Onboarding asks for name, not username. Sign-in uses email. The instructor display name is `Joshua`.\\n\\n## Smart Drill Note\\n\\nOn `/drill`, adaptive Smart Drill can show a `currentQuestion` that is not the same as `session.questionQueue[session.currentIndex]`. Any per-question UI state must key off `currentQuestion.qid` unless the code path is explicitly limited to queued drills.\\n\\n## Verification (must not skip)\\n\\n- Any change to `src/components/` or `src/pages/` is not \\"done\\" until it has been viewed running in the app (dev server + a browser tool), with a screenshot or DOM check as evidence. Never report a UI change complete on the strength of the diff alone. This is the single biggest recurring failure across past sessions — a hard gate, not a suggestion.\\n- **Responsive fixes carry a wider gate: verify across the full emulate matrix BEFORE pushing, not after Joshua reports it.** Narrow, mid, and wide viewports plus the sidecar-docked case, every time. A pushed \\"fix\\" that does not hold costs a second full session; this has already happened once.\\n\\n## Workflow Expectations\\n\\n- Run `npm run build` before wrapping up significant code or documentation work.\\n- If tests are relevant to the change, run them too.\\n- After completing a change, stage the intended files, commit with a concise message, and push to `origin main` unless the user says otherwise.\\n- **Push via `.claude/safe-push.sh`, not raw `git push`.** Lovable auto-commits to `main` mid-session, so a raw push can be rejected or race a concurrent commit. The script fetches, merges `origin/main` if it diverged (never rebases, never force-pushes), then pushes. It also gates on `npm run typecheck`.\\n- **Never roll `main` back.** Do not use Lovable\'s \\"Revert to commit\\", \\"revert and resend\\", or history restore, and never reset or force-push `main`. On 2026-09-27 an accidental \\"revert and resend\\" (b998596) silently undid three merged PRs across 121 question-bank files, and students saw the wrong questions again. To undo your own work, `git revert <your SHA>` only. If a Lovable dialog offers a revert, dismiss it; if one fires, stop and tell Joshua before repairing anything.\\n- Existing Codex repo hooks live in `.codex/hooks.json`; preserve them unless the task explicitly requires a hook change.\\n\\n## Session Protocol (second brain)\\n\\nProduct context, status, and decisions live in the Obsidian vault at `~/ai-os/`, not this repo. Two repo-local skills in `.agents/skills/` run the session ritual; the canonical protocol is in the vault `CLAUDE.md`.\\n\\nThe vault was rebuilt 2026-07-16 (paths relative to `~/ai-os`). Live: teaching voice `wiki/teaching-voice.md` (route ALL student-facing copy through it), the business `wiki/entities/aspiring-attorneys.md`, a student\'s record `students/<name>/` (see `students/README.md`), teaching knowledge `corpus/index.md`, and the `wiki/{tasks,hot,dashboard,index,log}.md` hubs named below. Archived read-only reference (pre-rebuild product knowledge, may be stale): `archive/wiki-pre-rebuild-2026-07-16/domains/lsat-u/` — product context `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-product.md`, design system `…/synthesis/intro-to-lr-design.md`, quality bar `…/synthesis/bootcamp-quality-bar.md`, domain index `…/index.md`.\\n\\n- `pickup` — start of session. Read `wiki/tasks.md` (the Now / Next / Done ledger) first, then `wiki/hot.md`, `wiki/dashboard.md`, skim `wiki/index.md`, read the latest `wiki/log.md` entry. Emit a 6-8 line briefing (where we left off, Now, Next, open threads, hot, suggested next) and wait. Do not deep-read other pages until pointed at a task.\\n- `wrapup` — end of session. Propose which `## Now` tasks finished and confirm before writing; a wrong \\"Done\\" is the failure to avoid. Then strike finished items through in `wiki/tasks.md` with a date, refresh `wiki/hot.md`, append a dated `wiki/log.md` entry (newest first, distinct label for same-day sessions), update the task anchor.\\n\\n`wiki/tasks.md` is the single source of truth for what is open vs done. Finished work is struck through so a new session never re-runs it. `raw/` in the vault is immutable.\\n\\n## Codex Adapter Layout\\n\\n- `.codex/config.toml`: minimal shared Codex project config. Keep secrets and machine-specific overrides out of this file.\\n- `.codex/agents/`: Codex agent definitions converted from `.claude/agents/*.md` when Claude agents exist. Use `.toml` files with converted instructions in `developer_instructions`.\\n- `.agents/skills/`: repository-local Codex skills copied from `.claude/skills/` when Claude skills exist and Codex should use them. Keep each `SKILL.md` with its supporting files.\\n- `.codex/hooks.json`: existing repository hooks. Preserve unless the task explicitly concerns hooks.\\n\\nThis checkout currently has Claude settings and hooks under `.claude/`, but no `.claude/skills/` or `.claude/agents/` directory to convert.\\n\\n## Known Gaps\\n\\n- Vitest specs exist (80 `src/**/*.test.ts(x)` files as of 2026-07-16); run with `npm run test`. Playwright is wired with one e2e spec (`e2e/smoke.spec.ts`), run via `npm run test:e2e`.\\n- No Prettier config and no CI workflows.\\n- `README.md` is still minimal.\\n\\n## Agent skills\\n\\n### Issue tracker\\n\\nGitHub Issues on `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db`, via the `gh` CLI. See `docs/agents/issue-tracker.md`.\\n\\n### Triage labels\\n\\nThe five canonical roles, unchanged: `needs-triage`, `needs-info`, `ready-for-agent`, `ready-for-human`, `wontfix`. See `docs/agents/triage-labels.md`.\\n\\n### Domain docs\\n\\nMulti-context: `CONTEXT-MAP.md` points at eight per-context glossaries; ADRs are all at the root. See `docs/agents/domain.md`.\\n"}'}]

[{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 2.1 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"62713b","wall_time_seconds":0.2369095,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2929,"output":"# Navigation/access #168 verification (Claude slice)\\n\\nDate: 2026-10-01\\nBaseline: 411267d3f4e899ee1228410decaf2578bc2da9b7 (branch `codex/navigation-access-168`)\\nStatus: Repaired on 2026-10-02. Historical builder checks below are superseded by the host repair record at the end. Whole #168 acceptance remains open.\\n\\n## Route matrix\\n\\nNavigation hints live in `src/lib/destinationAccess.ts`. Nested route gates remain the authority for access. Assignment-aware exam tabs stay reachable so TestingCentreGate can check actual assignments.\\n\\n| Destination | Bare URL lands on | Tabs (route gate) |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| Home `/classroom` | itself | allowSignedIn (classroom flag for coursework children) |\\n| My Work `/my-work` | first open tab, else To do | To do, Completed, Notes & recaps: `has_classroom_access` |\\n| Practice `/practice` | Drills; without drill: Overview when practice/Classroom/admin permits the gate to check access, otherwise Attempts | Drills: `has_drill_access`. Overview/All Exams: TestingCentreGate. Attempts: allowSignedIn |\\n| Review `/review` | To review | To review: allowSignedIn. Wrong answers: `has_journal_access`. Flagged: `has_flagged_access` |\\n| Progress `/progress` | Overview | Overview, History, Topics: `has_analytics_access` |\\n| Library `/library` | first open tab | Lessons: activity/bootcamp/drill/analytics. Activities, Bootcamps: `has_classroom_access`. Admissions: requireAdmin |\\n| Schedule `/schedule` | itself | allowSignedIn |\\n\\n- **Direct link and reload:** each tab is its own route, wrapped in its parent `ProtectedRoute allowSignedIn`. A reload re-resolves auth, then permissions, then the tab\'s gate.\\n- **Signed out:** the user goes to `/auth?returnTo=<path+query+hash>`.\\n- **Active state:** the rail sets `aria-current=\\"page\\"`. The tab row marks the owning tab on nested pages: `/practice/drills/runs/:id` lights Drills, and `/practice/exams/:pt` lights All Exams.\\n- **Query and hash:** bare destination URLs and legacy redirects carry both through.\\n\\n## Defects found and fixed\\n\\n1. **Stale proof test.** At baseline, `railCatalog.test.ts` still expected Library Activities to be gated on `has_activity_access`. The tier-defaults change (5b5d5b0) deliberately moved it to `has_classroom_access`. I updated the test to match that policy. The policy itself is unchanged.\\n2. **Locked rail items hid their explanation.** My Work and Progress rendered as `disabled` buttons with no text, even though their routes admit any signed-in account and the destination page explains each lock. A keyboard student could not focus them at all. Now:\\n   - Rail destinations are always navigable.\\n   - A padlock, a \\"(locked)\\" screen-reader label and a title appear only when every non-staff section is locked.\\n   - The rail and the Account layout preview use the same predicate (`railItemLocked`).\\n3. **The Library padlock was wrong for the current tiers.** The rail locked Library on `has_activity_access`/`has_bootcamp_access`, which tier defaults no longer grant, so a Classroom (Tier 2) student saw a lock on a destination they could open.\\n4. **Bare destination URLs landed on locked tabs.** For example, `/library` on a Classroom-only account opened a locked Lessons page. `DestinationIndex` waits while permissions are unresolved and preserves query/hash. The rail uses the same landing logic. Without drill access, Practice opens Overview when practice/Classroom/admin access lets TestingCentreGate consider entitlement or assignments. Otherwise it opens Attempts. Classroom access alone makes the gate reachable, but actual owned assignments still decide content access.\\n5. **Locked tabs gave no visible reason.** The only explanation was a `title` tooltip. Destination pages now show a visible note (\\"Sections with a lock aren\'t in your plan right now. If that looks wrong, message Joshua.\\"), and each locked tab is tied to it with `aria-describedby`. The staff-only Admissions tab says \\"open to Joshua only\\" and doesn\'t trigger the plan note.\\n6. **Legacy redirects dropped context.** `/waj` bounced through `/journal` and lost its query and hash. `/foyer`, `/mainhub`, `/profile` and `/settings/layout` dropped their queries, and `/bootcamp/structure(-v2)(/*)` took two hops through `/bootcamps`. All of these now go to the canonical target in one hop with the query and hash kept. An explicit target parameter still wins, so `/settings/layout` always opens `section=layout`. A test asserts that no `RedirectWithSearch` target is itself a redirect source, so no redirect can loop.\\n7. **Quick access shortcuts disappeared from keyboard order.** Locked Messages and Find a question used `disabled`, which removes them from the tab order. They now use `aria-disabled`: still focusable and inert, with a \\"(locked for your account)\\" label.\\n8. **Rail preference migration race.** If a second tab or device migrated between the read and the guarded write, the compare-and-swap correctly refused, but the hook then showed an error. It now re-reads and adopts the newer stored layout, so it neither migrates twice nor fails.\\n9. **Stale rail preference save.** A save built on a cached layout could overwrite a newer layout saved from another device, because the compare-and-swap only covered the gap between that save\'s own read and write. A save now refuses when the stored v2 layout differs from the layout the edit was based on, then reloads.\\n10. **Minor:** `/classroom/admissions` was briefly classified as a \\"work\\" section before its redirect fired. It is now excluded.\\n\\n## Changed files\\n\\n- `src/lib/destinationAccess.ts` (new): route matrix, tab access, landing path, lock state, active tab.\\n- `src/lib/destinationAccess.test.ts` (new): matrix and landing regressions.\\n- `src/pages/StudentDestination.tsx`: uses the matrix, adds `DestinationIndex` and the visible lock note.\\n- `src/pages/StudentDestination.test.tsx` (expanded) and `src/pages/StudentDestination.routes.test.tsx` (new). The routes test composes ProtectedRoute exactly as App.tsx does and covers auth loading, signed out, permission loading, denied, entitled, admin Admissions, and revoked states.\\n- `src/App.tsx`: legacy redirects and destination index routes only.\\n- `src/components/classroom/shell/ClassroomLayout.tsx` and `ClassroomLayout.test.tsx` (new): rail locks, shortcuts, `aria-current`, and mobile menu Escape and focus return.\\n- `src/components/classroom/classroom.css`: `.classroom-nav-dimmed` and `.student-destination-locked-note` only.\\n- `src/components/classroom/account/RailLayoutEditor.tsx`: preview uses the shared lock predicate.\\n- `src/hooks/useRailPrefs.ts` and its test: migration race and stale-save fixes, with 5 new tests.\\n- `src/lib/railCatalog.test.ts`: stale policy assertion fixed, and tab paths now read from the matrix.\\n- `src/components/ProtectedRoute.tsx` and its tests: staff-only denial wording and regression coverage.\\n- `src/components/LockedModule.tsx`: neutral unavailable-access copy, reachable message hint and an Attempts button on denied exam routes.\\n- `preserveRedirectLocation.ts`, `authRedirect.ts` and `useDialogOverlay.ts` are unchanged.\\n- Inherited from current main, outside this navigation authorship: `src/pages/teacher/desk/DeskPeopleSurface.tsx` from 99aa43d; `public/data/PT107-S3-LR.json` from 320de0d (#186); `public/data/PT149-S4-LR.json` from 231a474 (#187). This document is part of each commit.\\n\\n## Correction pass 1 (review of 3d2fb5e)\\n\\nThe independent review found that 3d2fb5e was not safe to release. Each finding, and what changed:\\n\\n| # | Finding | Correction or gate |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| 1a | `useBootcampProgress` replaced `metadata_json` with only `bootcamp_id` and `progress`, deleting `session_chain` on the next autosave. | It now calls the new `mergeClassroomItemMetadata` (`src/lib/classroomItems.ts`). Each attempt re-reads the row, rebuilds the metadata from the stored value plus `bootcamp_id`/`progress`, and writes status/`completed_at` in the same update. The update is conditioned on the `updated_at` just read; a concurrent write makes it match zero rows, so it re-reads and recomputes, up to 3 attempts. After that it throws `ClassroomItemMergeError` (the hook logs it, as before) and never falls back to an unconditional write. The effect now depends on `user.id`, not the `user` object. |\\n| 1b | `markNoteRead` spread the caller\'s cached metadata, so it could erase a link added later or restore one removed later. | It uses the same merge, recomputing `read_at` from the stored row. It skips the write when the stored row is already read. A failed merge rejects, and its only caller already treats that as non-fatal and claims nothing. |\\n| 1c | Other writers | All other `classroom_items` updates (`syncClassroomItemStatusBySource`, `completeAssignedWork`, `closeOutAssignedSection`, weekly-plan publish, access tier, `admin-manage-users` practice-test reassign, teacher bootcamp date edits) set named columns only, and Postgres leaves `metadata_json` untouched. The MCP edge function only reads. No other writer replaces metadata on an existing row. |\\n| 2 | A student may change `metadata_json` on their own rows (`guard_classroom_items_student_update` permits it; `classroom_items_student_update_status` checks only `student_id`). So `session_chain` can be inserted, moved or removed by the student account, and the teacher view could not prove teacher authorship. | **Gated.** `SESSION_LINK_WRITES_ENABLED = false` in `src/lib/sessionChain.ts`. The dossier panel shows **Link existing work** disabled, with `aria-describedby` pointing at: \\"Secure session linking is not available yet…\\". No Move or Remove control and no dialog renders, and `save` is never reached. Teacher-visible links carry \\"Links below are read from record data the student account can also edit. They are not verified as yours.\\" The appointment list says \\"(unverified)\\". Read-only Schedule/Notes views remain; they show only the signed-in student\'s own rows and bookings, which the server already scopes. The writer (`commitSessionLink`, `useSessionLinkWriter`) stays in code and tests for when the prerequisite below lands. |\\n| 3 | `SessionLinkedNotes` returned `null` on item-source failure. | Loading, booking failure, item failure (and both), stale-with-rows and stale-without-rows now render distinctly, inside the \\"From your sessions\\" section, with **Try again** wired to both refetches. |\\n| 4 | Canceled sessions were reachable only by deep link. | `SessionHistory` on Schedule lists past and canceled sessions from the same verified student booking source (`student-booking` `info`: every redemption status; single sessions = the latest 30). Each row has a \\"Session work\\" button that pushes the same `?session=v1.<source>.<id>` selection. Booking management is unchanged. New linking stays disabled for canceled appointments, and in any case while gated. |\\n| 5 | Teacher booking keys and writer epochs omitted the actor. | Teacher bookings and items now use feature-owned queries keyed `[\'session-chain\', \'teacher-bookings\' or \'teacher-items\', actorId, studentId]`. The shared `useStudentClassroomItems` is no longer used by this feature and is unchanged. The writer epoch resets on actor or student change; a result from the old context returns `superseded` and cannot clear the newer operation\'s pending state. `TeacherSessionWork` remounts on `actorId|studentId`, which drops drafts, dialogs and messages. |\\n| 6 | Extra blank line at EOF in `src/hooks/useSessionChain.ts`. | Removed. Whitespace is now checked against the committed diff from the base (below). |\\n\\n### Server prerequisite for re-enabling link writes (not applied)\\n\\nThe smallest additive change, for the host to apply through the approved Lovable route. No migration file is committed here.\\n\\n1. In `guard_classroom_items_student_update`, for non-admins also raise when `new.metadata_json -> \'session_chain\' IS DISTINCT FROM old.metadata_json -> \'session_chain\'`. Student progress and read writes keep working, because both writers now merge from the stored row and so carry the member through unchanged.\\n2. Add `set_classroom_item_session_link(_item_id uuid, _expected_updated_at timestamptz, _booking_source text, _booking_id uuid, _phase text)` (a null phase removes the link), `SECURITY DEFINER`, `search_path = public`, executable by `authenticated`, that:\\n   - requires `has_role(auth.uid(), \'admin\')`;\\n   - locks the item `FOR UPDATE` and returns a conflict when `updated_at <> _expected_updated_at`;\\n   - verifies booking ownership against the item\'s `student_id` (`booking_pass_redemptions` → `booking_passes.student_id`; `single_session_bookings.user_id`), and for new links that the booking status is `booked`;\\n   - validates the phase against the item type (recap ⇒ `session_recap`/`smartboard_recap`; preparation/follow-up ⇒ drill, homework, activity, bootcamp_module, practice_test, note);\\n   - sets only `metadata_json -> \'session_chain\'` via `jsonb_set`/`-`, adding `linked_by = auth.uid()` and `linked_at = now()`;\\n   - returns the updated row as the acknowledgment.\\n3. Then switch `commitSessionLink` to call that function, have the projection require `linked_by` for \\"verified\\" display, and flip `SESSION_LINK_WRITES_ENABLED`.\\n\\nUntil then, any `session_chain` already stored is legacy metadata of unproven authorship and is labelled that way.\\n\\n## Contract m…5623 tokens truncated… rejects that with `42501`. It is unreachable today (gated), but it must be switched to `set_classroom_item_session_link` before the flag is flipped.\\n2. **Checkout holds and failed bookings shown as sessions** (UX truthfulness; fixed). Unpaid holds would have appeared in \\"Past and canceled sessions\\" with a raw status such as \\"draft\\", and as teacher appointment rows. `toSessionBooking` now accepts only appointment statuses (`booked`, `cancelled`/`canceled`, `no_show`, `refund_pending`, `refunded`, `refund_failed`). Refund states read as Canceled and `no_show` reads as Missed.\\n3. **Silent omission beyond the 30-single-session cap** (truthfulness; fixed in UI, root cause open). A deep link or Notes link to an older single session read as \\"not available in your schedule\\". Now, when the student has 30 or more single-session rows, the unavailable state, the history list and Notes each say the coverage may be incomplete. Complete history needs `student-booking` to page (for example a `before` cursor) or to resolve specific linked ids. That is an edge-function change and is not done here.\\n4. **Verification label depends on the gate** (see the table). Must change before enabling.\\n5. **Pre-existing 10 px overflow** at 390 px with doubled text in the Schedule stage tabs and calendar header. Retained as an **unresolved acceptance item**: it is in existing Schedule chrome, appears with or without this feature, and fixing it means restyling navigation outside this ticket\'s surface.\\n\\n### Server contract (implemented as a proposal artifact, tested, not applied)\\n\\nFiles:\\n\\n- `docs/implementation-plans/artifacts/2026-10-02-session-chain-server-contract.sql`. It sits deliberately outside `supabase/migrations`, so merging this PR cannot apply it.\\n- `docs/implementation-plans/artifacts/2026-10-02-session-chain-server-contract-rollback.sql`.\\n- `scripts/verification/session-chain-server-contract.mjs`, a PGlite harness following the repo\'s existing `scripts/verification/*` convention (external `--pglite-module`; no app dependency added).\\n\\nDesign:\\n\\n- **Actor** is `auth.uid()` from the request JWT, never a parameter. **Authority** is `has_role(auth.uid(), \'admin\')`. The schema has only `admin` and `user` roles and no teacher-to-student roster, so \\"teacher\\" is the admin, exactly as `classroom_items_admin_all` already treats it.\\n- **Ownership:** the item\'s `student_id` must equal the intended `_student_id` (catches a stale UI selection). The booking must belong to that same student: a redemption via `booking_passes.student_id`; a single session via `single_session_bookings.user_id`, so email-only matches are rejected.\\n- **Eligibility:** the phase must fit the item type (Recap needs `session_recap`/`smartboard_recap`); archived items are refused; new links need booking status `booked`. Removal skips booking checks, so links on canceled appointments can still be removed.\\n- **Concurrency:** `SELECT … FOR UPDATE`, then compare `_expected_updated_at`. A mismatch returns `{status:\'conflict\', session_chain:<current>}` and writes nothing. A request for the state already stored returns `{status:\'unchanged\'}` with no write, so a retried lost reply is safe. A success returns `{status:\'saved\', updated_at, session_chain}` as the acknowledgment.\\n- **Preservation:** only `metadata_json -> \'session_chain\'` changes (`jsonb_set` or `-`); `linked_by` and `linked_at` are stamped.\\n- **All mutation paths:** a `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE` trigger rejects any change to that member unless it comes from inside the function. The function sets a transaction-local flag; PostgREST clients cannot set arbitrary settings. This covers student updates, admin direct updates, inserts and service-role writes. Writes that carry the member through unchanged pass: the note-read and bootcamp merges, status syncs, column-only admin edits.\\n- **No destructive cleanup:** existing members are copied into `classroom_item_session_link_legacy` (admin-read-only), and no row is rewritten. A legacy link becomes verified only when Joshua re-saves it through the function, which clears its legacy record.\\n- **Rollback:** drops the function, trigger and guard function. It leaves every row and, by default, the legacy record. Turn the frontend flag off first.\\n- **Known bypass, documented:** direct SQL as `postgres` (dashboard or migrations) can set the flag. That is outside the app\'s threat model.\\n\\nPGlite result: `PASS session_chain server contract: 52 checks`. Coverage:\\n\\n- the forge works before install; install rewrites no row and records the legacy member; install is re-runnable;\\n- student add, move and remove are rejected; student merge and status writes still work; a student calling the function, or a call with no JWT, is rejected;\\n- admin direct update and insert-with-member are rejected; admin column edits still work;\\n- rejected: another student\'s booking, the wrong intended student, a cross-student item, an email-match-only single session, a canceled booking for a new link, drill-as-recap, an unknown source, an unknown phase;\\n- a missing item returns `not_found`;\\n- save stamps `linked_by` from the JWT and preserves other metadata;\\n- an idempotent repeat makes no write; a stale `updated_at` returns a conflict with the current link and changes nothing;\\n- move and remove keep other keys; removal works from a canceled booking;\\n- legacy re-save verifies the link and clears the legacy record; the legacy table is admin-only;\\n- rollback leaves rows untouched and restores pre-contract behavior; the preconditions fail closed on the wrong baseline.\\n\\nPGlite is real Postgres compiled to WASM. It proves the SQL and trigger semantics, not Supabase\'s role and grant setup or PostgREST behavior.\\n\\nDeployment prerequisite, in order (none of this has been done):\\n\\n1. Joshua approves applying the SQL through the approved Lovable route, with **explicit confirmation immediately before any step that may spend Lovable credits**.\\n2. Pre-apply probe: `select count(*) from classroom_items where metadata_json ? \'session_chain\';`. Expect 0, since writes were never enabled. Any non-zero count is recorded as legacy, not deleted.\\n3. Apply. Post-apply probe:\\n   - `to_regprocedure(\'public.set_classroom_item_session_link(uuid,uuid,timestamptz,text,uuid,text)\')` is not null;\\n   - the trigger `classroom_items_session_chain_guard` exists;\\n   - a student-JWT `update … metadata_json || \'{\\"session_chain\\":{}}\'` on an owned fixture returns `42501`.\\n4. Frontend change, as a separate reviewed commit:\\n   - switch `commitSessionLink` to the RPC and map `saved`/`unchanged`/`conflict`/`not_found` onto the existing outcomes;\\n   - derive \\"verified\\" from `linked_by` plus the legacy table;\\n   - regenerate Supabase types;\\n   - then flip `SESSION_LINK_WRITES_ENABLED`.\\n5. Real-account acceptance (below).\\n\\n### Pass 2 changed files\\n\\n`src/lib/sessionChain.ts` (appointment-status filter, refund and no-show labels), `src/components/schedule/SessionChainPanel.tsx` (history filter, cap notes), `src/hooks/useSessionChain.ts` (`olderSingleSessionsOmitted`, `STUDENT_SINGLE_SESSION_CAP`), `src/pages/MyWorkNotesPage.tsx` (cap note). Tests: `src/lib/sessionChain.test.ts`, `src/pages/Schedule.sessionChain.test.tsx`, `src/components/schedule/SessionChainPanel.test.tsx` (fixture field). New: the two SQL artifacts and `scripts/verification/session-chain-server-contract.mjs`. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 5.6 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"03e483","wall_time_seconds":0.1449971,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2442,"output":"// Study settings: what each one is, where it is stored, and what it changes.\\n//\\n// Ownership and lifetime\\n// - Owner: the signed-in account (auth user id). Settings never follow a\\n//   device from one account to the next.\\n// - Persistence: this browser\'s localStorage, one key per account. There is\\n//   no server copy (no schema for one), so a second device starts from the\\n//   defaults. The UI says so.\\n// - Failure: a write that throws (private mode, full storage) leaves the\\n//   in-memory value in force for this tab and marks it unsaved. Later edits\\n//   build on that in-memory value, and a retry writes the latest value.\\n// - Legacy: before accounts were separated, every account on a device shared\\n//   one `userSettings` key. Nothing records which account wrote it, so it is\\n//   never attributed automatically. Every account starts from its own saved\\n//   settings or the defaults. When the old key holds non-default values, the\\n//   Account page offers them and only a person\'s explicit choice copies them\\n//   into their account. The old key is only ever read, never changed.\\n// - Unreadable storage: if the browser refuses to read the account\'s key,\\n//   defaults are used and the page says the saved settings could not be read.\\n//   A refused read is never treated as \\"no settings\\" for the legacy offer.\\n//\\n// Consequences\\n// - Timing is read when a section starts. A section already running keeps\\n//   the clock it started with; past sections keep the time they were taken\\n//   under.\\n// - Pool settings are read when a drill builds its question list. They\\n//   choose which questions come next and never edit past answers, scores or\\n//   history.\\n\\nexport type TimingMode = \'35\' | \'52.5\' | \'70\' | \'unlimited\';\\n\\nexport interface UserSettings {\\n  // Stored for compatibility. No screen in the app reads these five today,\\n  // so the Account page does not offer them as switches.\\n  tutorEnabled: boolean;\\n  voiceCoachEnabled: boolean;\\n  showContrast: boolean;\\n  teachBackOnCorrect: boolean;\\n  sectionDebriefEnabled: boolean;\\n  storeFullTranscript: boolean;\\n  // Live settings.\\n  defaultTimingMode: TimingMode;\\n  allowRepeats: boolean;\\n  preferUnseen: boolean;\\n  recycleAfterDays: number;\\n}\\n\\nexport const DEFAULT_SETTINGS: UserSettings = Object.freeze({\\n  tutorEnabled: true,\\n  voiceCoachEnabled: true,\\n  showContrast: false,\\n  teachBackOnCorrect: false,\\n  sectionDebriefEnabled: false,\\n  storeFullTranscript: false,\\n  defaultTimingMode: \'35\',\\n  allowRepeats: false,\\n  preferUnseen: true,\\n  recycleAfterDays: 30,\\n}) as UserSettings;\\n\\nexport const LEGACY_SETTINGS_KEY = \'userSettings\';\\nexport const RECYCLE_MIN_DAYS = 1;\\nexport const RECYCLE_MAX_DAYS = 365;\\n\\nexport function settingsKeyFor(userId: string): string {\\n  return `userSettings:${userId}`;\\n}\\n\\nconst TIMING_MODES: readonly TimingMode[] = [\'35\', \'52.5\', \'70\', \'unlimited\'];\\n\\nexport function isTimingMode(value: unknown): value is TimingMode {\\n  return typeof value === \'string\' && (TIMING_MODES as readonly string[]).includes(value);\\n}\\n\\n/** A recycle value is valid only as a whole number of days in range. */\\nexport function parseRecycleDays(raw: unknown): number | null {\\n  const n = typeof raw === \'number\' ? raw : typeof raw === \'string\' && raw.trim() !== \'\' ? Number(raw) : NaN;\\n  if (!Number.isInteger(n) || n < RECYCLE_MIN_DAYS || n > RECYCLE_MAX_DAYS) return null;\\n  return n;\\n}\\n\\n/**\\n * Field-by-field repair of anything read from storage. A bad field falls back\\n * to its default; good fields survive. Unknown keys are dropped.\\n */\\nexport function normalizeSettings(raw: unknown): UserSettings {\\n  const src = raw && typeof raw === \'object\' && !Array.isArray(raw) ? (raw as Record<string, unknown>) : {};\\n  const bool = (k: keyof UserSettings) =>\\n    typeof src[k] === \'boolean\' ? (src[k] as boolean) : (DEFAULT_SETTINGS[k] as boolean);\\n  // Numbers were once stored as strings by a form; accept \\"52.5\\" and 52.5.\\n  const timing = typeof src.defaultTimingMode === \'number\' ? String(src.defaultTimingMode) : src.defaultTimingMode;\\n  return {\\n    tutorEnabled: bool(\'tutorEnabled\'),\\n    voiceCoachEnabled: bool(\'voiceCoachEnabled\'),\\n    showContrast: bool(\'showContrast\'),\\n    teachBackOnCorrect: bool(\'teachBackOnCorrect\'),\\n    sectionDebriefEnabled: bool(\'sectionDebriefEnabled\'),\\n    storeFullTranscript: bool(\'storeFullTranscript\'),\\n    defaultTimingMode: isTimingMode(timing) ? timing : DEFAULT_SETTINGS.defaultTimingMode,\\n    allowRepeats: bool(\'allowRepeats\'),\\n    preferUnseen: bool(\'preferUnseen\'),\\n    recycleAfterDays: parseRecycleDays(src.recycleAfterDays) ?? DEFAULT_SETTINGS.recycleAfterDays,\\n  };\\n}\\n\\n/**\\n * saved       the account\'s own stored settings\\n * default     the account has no stored settings yet\\n * unreadable  the account\'s key exists but is not valid JSON\\n * unavailable the browser refused to read storage at all\\n */\\nexport type SettingsSource = \'saved\' | \'default\' | \'unreadable\' | \'unavailable\';\\n\\ntype StorageLike = Pick<Storage, \'getItem\' | \'setItem\'>;\\n\\n/** `undefined` means the read itself failed, which is not the same as absent. */\\nfunction safeGet(storage: StorageLike | null, key: string): string | null | undefined {\\n  if (!storage) return undefined;\\n  try {\\n    return storage.getItem(key);\\n  } catch {\\n    return undefined;\\n  }\\n}\\n\\nfunction sameSettings(a: UserSettings, b: UserSettings): boolean {\\n  return (Object.keys(DEFAULT_SETTINGS) as (keyof UserSettings)[]).every((k) => a[k] === b[k]);\\n}\\n\\nfunction parse(json: string): unknown {\\n  try {\\n    return JSON.parse(json);\\n  } catch {\\n    return undefined;\\n  }\\n}\\n\\n/**\\n * Read an account\'s settings. Never throws, and never applies the old\\n * device-wide settings. `legacyOffer` is set only when the account has\\n * definitely stored nothing yet and the old key holds readable, non-default\\n * values; the person decides whether to use them.\\n */\\nexport function readSettingsFor(\\n  storage: StorageLike | null,\\n  userId: string | null,\\n): { settings: UserSettings; source: SettingsSource; legacyOffer: UserSettings | null } {\\n  const defaults = { ...DEFAULT_SETTINGS };\\n  if (!userId) return { settings: defaults, source: \'default\', legacyOffer: null };\\n  const own = safeGet(storage, settingsKeyFor(userId));\\n  if (own === undefined) return { settings: defaults, source: \'unavailable\', legacyOffer: null };\\n  if (own !== null) {\\n    const value = parse(own);\\n    return value === undefined\\n      ? { settings: defaults, source: \'unreadable\', legacyOffer: null }\\n      : { settings: normalizeSettings(value), source: \'saved\', legacyOffer: null };\\n  }\\n  return { settings: defaults, source: \'default\', legacyOffer: readLegacyOffer(storage) };\\n}\\n\\n/** The old device-wide settings, when readable and different from defaults. */\\nexport function readLegacyOffer(storage: StorageLike | null): UserSettings | null {\\n  const raw = safeGet(storage, LEGACY_SETTINGS_KEY);\\n  if (typeof raw !== \'string\') return null;\\n  const value = parse(raw);\\n  if (value === undefined) return null;\\n  const settings = normalizeSettings(value);\\n  return sameSettings(settings, DEFAULT_SETTINGS) ? null : settings;\\n}\\n\\n/** Write an account\'s settings. Returns false instead of throwing. */\\nexport function writeSettingsFor(storage: StorageLike | null, userId: string, settings: UserSettings): boolean {\\n  if (!storage) return false;\\n  try {\\n    storage.setItem(settingsKeyFor(userId), JSON.stringify(settings));\\n    return true;\\n  } catch {\\n    return false;\\n  }\\n}\\n\\n/** Plain summary of an offer, so a person can recognise their own settings. */\\nexport function legacyOfferSummary(s: UserSettings): string {\\n  const parts = [`section time ${TIMING_LABEL[s.defaultTimingMode]}`];\\n  parts.push(s.allowRepeats ? \'repeats allowed\' : \'no repeats\');\\n  parts.push(s.preferUnseen ? \'new questions first\' : \'new questions not first\');\\n  parts.push(`questions come back after ${s.recycleAfterDays} day${s.recycleAfterDays === 1 ? \'\' : \'s\'}`);\\n  return parts.join(\', \');\\n}\\n\\n/* ── consequences, in the words the Account page shows ─────────────────── */\\n\\nexport const TIMING_LABEL: Record<TimingMode, string> = {\\n  \'35\': \'35:00, standard time\',\\n  \'52.5\': \'52:30, time and a half\',\\n  \'70\': \'70:00, double time\',\\n  unlimited: \'No timer, a stopwatch counts up\',\\n};\\n\\nexport function timingConsequence(mode: TimingMode): string {\\n  const clock =\\n    mode === \'unlimited\'\\n      ? \'Timed sections you start will run without a countdown.\'\\n      : `Timed sections you start will give you ${mode === \'35\' ? \'35 minutes\' : mode === \'52.5\' ? \'52 minutes 30 seconds\' : \'70 minutes\'} as your standard section.`;\\n  return `${clock} Practice-test paces are worked out from this too. A section already running keeps its clock, and past sections keep the time they were taken under.`;\\n}\\n\\nexport function poolConsequence(s: Pick<UserSettings, \'allowRepeats\' | \'preferUnseen\' | \'recycleAfterDays\'>): string {\\n  const days = `${s.recycleAfterDays} day${s.recycleAfterDays === 1 ? \'\' : \'s\'}`;\\n  if (s.allowRepeats && !s.preferUnseen) {\\n    return \'Your next drill can draw from every question, including ones you have already answered.\';\\n  }\\n  if (s.allowRepeats && s.preferUnseen) {\\n    return \'Your next drill uses questions you have never answered while any are left, then draws from every question.\';\\n  }\\n  if (s.preferUnseen) {\\n    return `Your next drill uses questions you have never answered while any are left. After that, a question comes back ${days} after you last saw it.`;\\n  }\\n  return `Your next drill mixes questions you have never answered with ones you last saw more than ${days} ago.`;\\n}\\n\\nexport const POOL_HISTORY_NOTE =\\n  \'These settings only choose which questions come next. Your past answers, scores and history do not change. Full sections and hand-picked question sets are always served whole.\';\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"232f32","wall_time_seconds":1.621238,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":2015,"output":"src/hooks/useWeeklyPlanPublisher.ts:47:  const viewRef = React.useRef({ teacherId: currentTeacherId, studentId: currentStudentId, epoch: 0, actorEpoch: 0 });\\nsrc/hooks/useWeeklyPlanPublisher.ts:53:      actorEpoch: viewRef.current.actorEpoch + Number(viewRef.current.teacherId !== currentTeacherId),\\nsrc/hooks/useWeeklyPlanPublisher.ts:63:    // Only the current teacher can start or retry a job, and only their own.\\nsrc/hooks/useWeeklyPlanPublisher.ts:87:      const sameActor = now.teacherId === job.teacherId && now.actorEpoch === startedIn.actorEpoch;\\nsrc/hooks/useWeeklyPlanPublisher.ts:102:  const retry = (studentId: string) => {\\nsrc/hooks/useWeeklyPlanPublisher.ts:108:  /** Stop offering a retry. raw.selected_answer : null, at: raw.created_at, item };\\n}\\n\\nexport function parseHardSentencesCapstone(raw: unknown, key: string, userId: string): HardSentencesAttempt | null {\\n  if (!object(raw) || raw.calibration_module !== \'M5\') return null;\\n  const item = M5_CAPSTONE.find(candidate => candidate.id === raw.item_id);\\n  if (!item) {\\n    if (typeof raw.item_id === \'string\' && raw.item_id.startsWith(\'MC-HS-CAP-\')) throw new Error(\'Saved Hard Sentences capstone wording is unavailable.\');\\n    return null;\\n  }\\n  if (raw.user_id !== userId || raw.calibration_only !== true || typeof raw.correct !== \'boolean\' || !date(raw.attempted_at)\\n    || typeof raw.picked !== \'string\' || !item.choices.some(choice => choice.letter === raw.picked)) throw new Error(\'Invalid saved Hard Sentences capstone attempt.\');\\n  if (raw.correct) return null;\\n  // Older producers used distinct envelope and payload UUIDs. The envelope is the exact fetch key.\\n  return { kind: \'capstone\', key, answer: raw.picked, at: raw.attempted_at, item };\\n}\\n\\nexport function hardSentencesQueueItem(attempt: HardSentencesAttempt): ReviewQueueItem {\\n  return {\\n    key: `hard-sentences:${attempt.kind}:${attempt.key}`, area: \'lr\', qid: attempt.item.id,\\n    label: `Hard Sentences · ${attempt.kind === \'cluster\' ? \'Cluster drill\' : \'Capstone\'} · ${attempt.item.id}`,\\n    qtype: attempt.kind === \'cluster\' ? \'Cluster decomposition\' : \'Cluster decomposition capstone\', reasons: [\'missed\'], sources: [\'hard_sentences\'],\\n    firstAnswer: null, latestAnswer: attempt.answer, correctAnswer: null,\\n    firstMissAt: attempt.at, lastActivityAt: attempt.at, reflection: null, flaggedNote: null,\\n    href: `/review/hard-sentences/${attempt.kind}/${encodeURIComponent(attempt.key)}`, actionLabel: \'Review saved attempt\',\\n  };\\n}\\n\\nasync function loadCluster(userId: string): Promise<ReviewQueueItem[]> {\\n  const items: ReviewQueueItem[] = [];\\n  for (let offset = 0; ; offset += 500) {\\n    const { data, error } = await supabase.from(\'intro_lr_attempts\').select(\'*\').eq(\'user_id\', userId)\\n      .eq(\'drill_id\', HS_DRILL_ID).eq(\'correct\', false).order(\'id\').range(offset, offset + 499);\\n    if (error) throw error;\\n    if (!Array.isArray(data)) throw new Error(\'Hard Sentences cluster source returned an invalid response.\');\\n    for (const raw of data) { const attempt = parseHardSentencesCluster(raw, userId); ; offset += 500) {\\n    const { data, error } = await supabase.from(\'bootcamp_kv\').select(\'record_id,data\').eq(\'user_id\', userId)\\n      .eq(\'table_name\', \'calibration_attempts\').order(\'record_id\').range(offset, offset + 499);\\n    if (error) throw error;\\n    if (!Array.isArray(data)) throw new Error(\'Hard Sentences capstone source returned an invalid response.\');\\n    for (const row of data) {\\n      if (!row || typeof row.record_id !== \'string\') throw new Error(\'Invalid saved capstone envelope.\');\\n      const attempt = parseHardSentencesCapstone(row.data, row.record_id, userId);\\n      if (attempt) items.push(hardSentencesQueueItem(attempt));\\n    }\\n    if (data.length < 500) return items;\\n  }\\n}\\n\\nexport async function loadHardSentencesReview(userId: string): Promise<HardSentencesReviewResult> {\\n  const results = await Promise.allSettled([loadCluster(userId), loadCapstone(userId)]);\\n  return {\\n    items: results.flatMap(result => result.status === \'fulfilled\' ? result.value : []).sort((a, b) => b.lastActivityAt.localeCompare(a.lastActivityAt)),\\n    unavailable: results.flatMap((result, index) => result.status === \'rejected\' ? Whole #170 remains open.\\n\\n## Scope\\n\\nThis slice is isolated from Claude\'s #171 drill-builder ownership and the pending #176 session-link deployment. It changes only Review queue/detail/client-contract code, tests and verification helpers. No backend deployment, AI inference, database mutation, bank edit, historical-answer rewrite or flag removal occurred.\\n\\nActivity queue/detail now require completed status plus a valid completion timestamp, and accept only attributable judged responses with valid attempt identity. The detail page verifies the exact owner/assignment, hides stale account/route data before effects settle, and offers Retry on read failure.\\n\\nThe saved review service validates episode/event identities, supported enums, timestamps, text payloads, student event ownership, unique immutable events and exact scope before interpreting completion. An independent reviewer caught a new incompatibility with server-valid empty reflection payloads; it was removed and both historical forms have regression coverage.\\n\\nReview adds URL-backed area/source filters, searches displayed flag notes, and retains the last-opened item key alongside query/reason/view/pagination in its detail return URL. Returning marks the exact row Last opened. Changing filters clears stale selection and pagination. Source failures explicitly describe the count as partial.\\n\\n## Automated evidence\\n\\n- Final related suite: 87/87 tests across 12 files.\\n- Typecheck: passed.\\n- Scoped ESLint: passed, no warnings or errors.\\n- Final production build: passed, 7138 modules and 769 canonical-output checks.\\n- Diff whitespace check: passed.\\n- Generated bank/address-index/MCP churn restored before staging.\\n- Independent source re-review approved after the reflection correction.\\n\\n## Actual authenticated browser evidence\\n\\nNormal teacher sign-in used existing locally configured credentials. Credentials are absent from this record. The actual app at port8184 displayed 282 owned Review items.\\n\\nSelecting Wrong-answer journal, LR, search PT159-S1-Q10 by its displayed address, and detailed table produced one owned row. Opening its exact journal route and using Back to review queue retained source/area/search/table and its last-opened key. Reload preserved the controls and marker.
